1Palm Beach investigation → 2008 NPAConfirmed

Verification: V

Span: Mar 2005–Jun 2008; documented record to Aug 24, 2011. Anchors (slice-attributed): DS9 — EFTA00231917 (1,120 pp probable-cause affidavit, Case #05-368), EFTA00058429, EFTA00077923, EFTA00240536, EFTA00227381 (2,265 pp case file), EFTA00193199 (Jun 2008 prosecutor emails), EFTA00039421; DS1–8 — EFTA00007157, EFTA00009448, EFTA00009016, plus EFTA00009478, EFTA00009502, EFTA00009440, EFTA00009512 (May 15, 2007 grand-jury testimony), EFTA00007253; HO — 8514, 8898, 8461, 11085, 2152, 6027, plus 1690–1693, 2150, 2148, 8614. Endpoint: 2008 non-prosecution agreement / state plea (HO stopping point Jun 30, 2008, HO 6027). Caveat: the signed NPA text itself was not re-verified in the DS9 sweep; the endpoint rests on the June 2008 negotiation emails + case file, and in the DS1–8 slice the NPA is discussed only retrospectively (closing doc is the undated-in-slice Acosta deposition). Links: 2 (breach), P1 (CVRA), P14 (Ruemmler counsel), P2 (Florida supervision). v2.0 DS8 extension: the plea hearing itself — certified transcript of the June 30, 2008 plea conference (EFTA00027591); Nov 21, 2005 sworn statement of the Palm Beach house manager (EFTA00029390); Aug 22, 2006 interview of a former residence employee, resurfaced via Oct 4, 2021 email (EFTA00029223); 1B4 evidence-box log, last inventoried Jul 20, 2021 (EFTA00029907); the NPA instrument itself (EFTA00013680) and the state plea form (EFTA00013890); plea-hearing transcript (EFTA00032238) — its cover/body show Jun 20 vs Jun 30, 2008; RESOLVED in v3.0 (WS5): the plea hearing was Jun 30, 2008 — both copies' cover pages give Jun 30, corroborated by EFTA00013678 (plea reported the afternoon of Jun 30) and EFTA00010507 (Marra opinion: plea entered Jun 30, 2008). Jun 20 appears only in the transcript's own page-2 preamble and is an internal error in the document, not a second hearing date. A DS11 copy (EFTA02729757, 51 pp.) shows the same cover/body discrepancy.

v3.0 DS11 extension: EFTA02729079 (Apr 17, 2006, 1 p.) — State Attorney Krischer's office confirms a settlement offer to Epstein's counsel: the state will not proceed with grand-jury proceedings the next day if Epstein pleads to aggravated assault with intent to commit a felony (third-degree), adjudication withheld, 5 years' probation, covering all allegations in PBPD case 05-000368; acceptance was to be confirmed in writing and is not established by this letter. EFTA02728515 (Aug 7, 2006, 1 p.) — Palm Beach PD request for a subpoena to Cingular Cellular for subscriber information and calls, Jan–Mar 2004, case 05-368. EFTA02729757 (51 pp.) — plea-conference transcript, State v. Epstein, certified copy dated June 30, 2008 with the proceedings body dated June 20, 2008 (see the resolved date discrepancy above). EFTA02729808 (21 pp.) and EFTA02725847 (11 pp.) — the negotiated plea terms sheet (two DS11 copies of the same form). EFTA02725185 (Nov 2009, 2 pp. — AUSA letter, the USAO's account of the Rothstein fraud as it touched the Epstein cases; the AUSA's characterizations, labeled as such; also a Story 6 extension).

v3.2 (WS5) extension — OCR-tolerant sweep legs: EFTA00186672 (DS9): grand-jury subpoena to JEGE Inc, 103 Foulk Road, Wilmington, DE, FGJ 05-02 (2005), subpoena to testify before the grand jury. Found via the CARBYNE group ("Carbine" hit is aircraft-registration OCR noise); the subpoena itself is a 2005 grand-jury item not in Story 1's anchors. EFTA00186947 (DS9): subpoena to testify before the grand jury, to the Custodian of Records, Broward Center for the Performing Arts, 201 SW Fifth Avenue, Fort Lauderdale (2007). EFTA00279466 (DS9): PBPD evidence inventory (CDs, phone messages, digital photos; case 05-368). A single inventory list; adds the evidence-handling detail to Story 1.

v3.3 (WS1) extension — negotiation chronology, filing record, and the record to Aug 2011: the NPA's signing mechanics and signature-page questions are documented to Feb 2010, and the documented record now runs to Aug 24, 2011 (SDFL office-wide recusal) via the timeline exhibit. Sep 24, 2007 — the signing-day transmission: AUSA transmits "The final Epstein agreement (I hope)" (EFTA00215028), appeals having reviewed the first "It appearing" paragraph, signed copy by fax, originals by FedEx. The negotiation chronology is indexed in "In Re Operation Leap Year Key Correspondence Volume 1 of 2 (Through Signing of NPA) INDEX" (EFTA00190101) — tab-level chronology Nov 16, 2006–Sep 24, 2007 (Vol 1, tabs 1–81) and post-signing (Vol 2), incl. the Jul 26, 2007 USAO defense summary of terms, Sep 10 Lefcourt's first proposed NPA, Sep 11 Acosta edits, Sep 12 Lefkowitz rejecting a maritime-assault plea, Sep 14 Acosta summary (plead to an Information: 1 count obstruction + 1 count simple assault), Sep 18 Lefkowitz's §1512(d) proposal and 12-month + community-control ask, Sep 19 Lefkowitz redline (waive PSI, binding sentencing recommendation, jointly recommend an incorrect Guidelines calculation, state-court Trust, plead to "coercing a person to become a prostitute", remove appeal waiver), Sep 21 registration dispute, Sep 23 defense admission of a "mistaken assumption" behind the 796.03 plea, Sep 24 final edits 1:54 p.m., signed agreement 4:26 p.m. The index is a finding aid, not the underlying letters — cite as the index's record of the correspondence. Sep 21, 2007 — Epstein "has agreed to all the terms, including pleading to the indictment, 18 months incarceration, followed by 1 year house arrest, as a condition of 5 years probation… plus a fund for the victims as Feds require. The sticking point is requiring a plea to a registerable offense" (EFTA00215169); same file: AUSA to Krischer — deadline of tomorrow afternoon for a signed agreement "or I need to indict the case on Tuesday"; defense "only want to plead to state charges, not concurrent state and federal". Sep 18, 2007 — "I know that the U.S. Attorney will not go below 18 months of prison/jail time" (EFTA00215281); Lefkowitz's alternative: one count of §1512, 12 months + 1 year home detention, then 2 years state probation. Sep 7, 2007 — AUSA circulates both drafts, "OLY_Plea_Agreement.wpd" + "OLY_Non-Prosecution_Agreement.wpd" (EFTA00013495). Sep 10, 2007 — AUSA → Lefcourt attaching the Office's written counterproposal (EFTA00067241). Jun 23, 2008 DOJ letter (Senior Associate Deputy AG) to Lefkowitz/Starr — DOJ completed "a thorough review of the U.S. Attorney's handling"; "we see nothing in the conduct of the U.S. Attorney's Office that gives us any reason to alter our opinion"; federal prosecution "appropriate" (EFTA00179250, which also carries the Dec 5, 2008 Acosta work-release notice to Brad Edwards — USAO learned of the work-release application only after Epstein had begun it, "more than six weeks after he began participating" — and the Nov 2, 2010 CVRA litigation-hold letter). Dec 4, 2007 — Acosta's reply to Starr's Nov 28, 2007 letter (EFTA00190007): the NPA's three federal interests — (1) plead guilty to a "registerable" offense, (2) binding recommendation for a sufficient term of imprisonment, (3) not harm victims' interests — "place the victims in the same position as they would have been, had Mr. Epstein been convicted at trial. No more; no less"; "the language of Paragraph 8 was [provided] to us by defense counsel." Feb 16–17, 2010 — "We have 'umpteen' copies of the NPA, but cannot find any that contain that signature to either the Non-Prosecution Agreement or the Addendum" (EFTA00213342); the signed addendum is transmitted separately (EFTA00207077, file dated Oct 30, 2007; the Second Addendum's recital says Oct 29, 2007 — both dates recorded as written, not reconciled). Jun 27, 2008 — Acosta → Goldberger/Black: the proposed state plea "does not comply with the terms of the Non-Prosecution Agreement" (18 months community control vs the NPA's consecutive 12 + 6 months county jail "without probation or community control in lieu of imprisonment") (EFTA00067456). Jul 8, 2008 — Acosta → Goldberger: on Jun 30, 2008 USAO provided a list of 31 individuals "whom it was prepared to name in an Indictment as victims"; Jul 7, another victim identified = 32 total; victim notifications to go out Jul 9, 2008 (EFTA00067249). Jul 9, 2008 — Goldberger → AUSA: objections to the victim notification (no hand service; no redacted list; eliminate the "acknowledgment" section; add the prior representation that the government is not vouching for any claim's veracity) (EFTA00067468). Jul 1, 2008 — AUSA attaches "the agreement, with addenda, for filing with the Court under seal" + flags "misstatements" during the change of plea (Florida Science Foundation incorporated Nov 2007, not "a couple of years ago"; its "office" address is Goldberger's suite) (EFTA00013914). Aug 12, 2008 — "It looks like they only filed the original September agreement not either of the addenda" (EFTA00013965). Sep 17, 2008 — AUSA → Krischer: the NPA's confidentiality provision "requires us to inform Mr. Epstein's counsel before making any disclosure — even a compulsory disclosure" (EFTA00014172). Nov 1, 2007 — DRAFT email to the State Attorney's Office: simultaneous plea + sentencing in Oct (later Nov), self-surrender by Jan 4, 2008 (EFTA00013523 — a draft, not a sent letter). The "EPSTEIN INVESTIGATION TIMELINE" exhibit (EFTA00224943, 51 pp., 6(e) material) — dated chronology May 1, 2006–Aug 24, 2011: May 1, 2006 Reiter letter; May 23, 2006 Operation Leap Year file opening; Aug 2, 2006 subpoenas; Nov 13, 2006 subpoenas to pilots; Jun 12, 2009 Marra hearing transcript; Sep 1, 2009 draft breach letter; Sep 29, 2009 letter re Epstein violating his no-contact order on Sep 16, 2009; Oct 29, 2009 "Open file and request USAO# (2009R02690, Operation Stolen Globe)"; Jul 1–31, 2010 Epstein completed house arrest; Sep 8, 2010 administrative order closing case; Mar 20, 2011 Acosta letter; Aug 2011 SDFL office-wide recusal notices (Jul 28–Aug 29, 2011). The CVRA exhibit "JEFFREY EPSTEIN Timeline-Summary" (EFTA00192978, 5 pp.) — meetings Feb 20, Jun 26, Jul 26, Sep 7, Sep 12, 2007; Sep 24, 2007 NPA signed; Sep 25, 2007 scheduled date for federal indictment; Oct 29, 2007 deadline for plea/sentencing; Jan 4, 2008 scheduled self-surrender; plus a Federal Sentencing Guidelines calculation (§§1591, 2422(b), 2423(b); offense level 36; advisory range 188–235 months) for the contemplated federal charges. Defense-side summaries — cite ONLY as Epstein's counsel's account, not as findings: EFTA00314859 ("SUMMARY": Mar 2005 PBPD investigation; the SAO prosecutor quoted as stating there were "no real victims"; the claim "the government agreed that there had never been an underage girl on Mr. Epstein's planes") and EFTA00731146 ("SUMMARY OF MISCONDUCT ISSUES": the defense's misconduct complaint against the USAO). FBI interview notes, PBPD-side account (speaker not identified in the text layer): Epstein donated $40,000 for a machine to help PBPD review security footage and wrote a $90,000 check for a fingerprint machine (not cashed) — "around the same time that the first victim had come forward" (EFTA00153737). Dec 3, 2008 — FBI Legal Attaché (Canberra) → Australian Federal Police: USAO West Palm Beach requests AFP assistance delivering a letter to an Australian-based victim referencing the NPA outcome + terms (EFTA00269841). Mar 11, 2011 — FBI EC: Mar 9, 2011 interview at USAO Fort Lauderdale with a victim + attorneys + private investigator; she "is willing to cooperate fully with any FBI investigation involving JEFFREY EPSTEIN and others" (EFTA00269912 — same Mar 9, 2011 interview as in EFTA01249058; shared with Story 2 and P46).

v3.4 (WS4) extension — victim-summary sheets: a run of eight redacted one-page victim-summary sheets from the Palm Beach investigation survives in DS9 (EFTA00216378 read in full as the exemplar; EFTA00216382, EFTA00216383, EFTA00216384, EFTA00216385, EFTA00216386, EFTA00216388, EFTA00216390 read in full as siblings). Each sheet records the girl's school at the time, the years, the number of sessions, and how she was brought to Epstein's house; the session counts in the run range from one to "over 100 times." No names are legible in the text layer and none is reproduced. The sheets are the investigation's victim-account record at summary level; per-sheet contents are the sheets' own record, not findings about any individual.

3SDNY federal case 2019Confirmed

Verification: V-C

Span: 2019. Anchors: EFTA02731039, EFTA00008529, EFTA02730486, EFTA02731082; DS9 match-only EFTA00040478, EFTA00040495; plus EFTA00008585 (v3.0, WS4: EFTA01000647 removed — Nov 11, 2014 email, 'ok send her to teterboro', a 2014 message with no connection to the 2019 SDNY case), EFTA00008599, EFTA00005718, EFTA02731069, EFTA02730741, EFTA00009658. Endpoint: indictment → arrest → death Aug 10, 2019 (per EFTA02730486) → post-death associate inquiry. Dated stopping point: Dec 19, 2019 (EFTA02731082); associate inquiry open. Links: 4, 5, 10, 11. v2.0 DS8 extension: the sealed 19 Cr. 490 indictment itself, filed Jul 2, 2019 (EFTA00009809); Jul 8, 2019 detention conference (EFTA00015606); victim outreach pre- and post-arrest, incl. May 24–25, 2019 Florida victim interviews (EFTA00015293) and Apr 16, 2021 / Aug 19–20, 2021 New York victim meetings (EFTA00009834, EFTA00010386); associate-inquiry reverse proffers Jul 2019 (EFTA00014508) and executed proffer agreement Nov 2020 (EFTA00015006); 9 East 71st St search-warrant affidavit, sworn Jul 2019 (EFTA00028388; duplicate EFTA00028499); Apr 5, 2019 attorney proffer (EFTA00030151); Santa Monica witness interviews Dec 15–17, 2019 (EFTA00024509); nolle prosequi hearing Aug 27, 2019 — 17 victims spoke or had statements read, plus 7 more (EFTA00019994, EFTA00031395, EFTA00031397); Sep 10, 2019 USVI-devices/six-binders warrants (EFTA00030708); Prince Andrew MLAT pursuit stated as intent, not a completed request (EFTA00027424); FBI summary Jul 17, 2024 (EFTA00038617); seized-device data-transfer saga Mar–Jul 2020 (20–40 TB); SDNY internal emails within hours of the death, incl. the forfeiture pivot (EFTA00015657; see new Story P20).

v3.0 DS11 extension: EFTA02730048 (Jul 3, 2019) — arrest / search-warrant stand-by list: 12–24 hours' notice expected when Epstein flies into the country; rendezvous at Teterboro; AUSA brief at noon Monday if the arrest has not happened. EFTA02730050 / EFTA02730052 (Jul 9, 2019) — NY agents/TFOs in the Tampa area of responsibility. EFTA02730067 / EFTA02730069 (Jul 11, 2019) — 'victim meeting' forwards with a search-warrant draft for agent review. EFTA02730005 / EFTA02730010 / EFTA02730014 (Aug 11–13, 2019) — EAN (travel) authority for case-related travel, St. Thomas. EFTA02730027 (Aug 14, 2019) — evidence load-up (FBI New York Rapid Deployment Team). EFTA02730034 (Aug 9, 2019) — St. Thomas search warrant under judicial review. EFTA02730071 (Sept 17, 2019) — status meeting with the ADIC: victims interviewed/total, spreadsheet tracking, CART and taint-review percentages, 3 proffers last week, 4 Florida victim interviews last week and ~5–6 planned in California.

v3.2 (WS2) extension — the April 3, 2020 MLAT request seeking Prince Andrew's testimony (shared with P13; full text in P13's entry above): EFTA00022062 (the request itself, Apr 3, 2020, "Material Witness PA", two investigations); EFTA00032129 (draft Mar 30, 2020); EFTA00037454 (final approved version sent Apr 3, 2020, 7:30 p.m.); EFTA00031512 (follow-up Jun 26–30, 2020); EFTA00029796 (signed final circulated Jun 25, 2020). Whether the interview occurred is NOT established in DS8.

v3.2 (WS2) extension — 13 legs: LSJ search-warrant affidavit (EFTA00020141, 24 pp.): FBI agent affidavit under Rule 41, U.S. District Court of the Virgin Islands, for Little St. James Island (75 acres, ~4 miles off St. Thomas), any buildings/structures and closed containers therein; sworn before Magistrate Judge Ruth Miller in August 2019 (day not legible in the text read; the affidavit bears a "2017.08.02" footer stamp, a PDF/template artifact, not the affidavit's date — it cites flights through June 11, 2019). Probable cause as stated: international flight records show a private jet owned and utilized by Epstein traveled to or from the USVI on April 14, May 5, May 18, and June 11, 2019; the affidavit states there is probable cause to believe Epstein "recently has been present at the Subject Premises." Items to be seized include documents/communications with or regarding victims or potential victims, photographs of victims or co-conspirators, nude/partially nude or sexually suggestive photographs of individuals who appear to be teenage girls or younger, documents evidencing co-conspirators' presence, and computer devices/storage media (or copies). LSJ execution-day brief (EFTA00019669, 13 pp., Aug 12, 2019): FBI New York, Criminal Division Branch C, Squad C-20, case 31E-NY-3027571 — "On 12 AUGUST 2019, FBI NYO Criminal Branch C will execute the search warrant for Little Saint James Island (LSJ), USVI, USA." Written as a pre-execution brief, so the completed search is documented as planned-and-briefed for August 12, not by a post-search return in DS8. Silent hit enabling the arrest (EFTA00037742, 1 p.): July 5, 2019 — "SILENT HIT" on an advance passenger information query: Epstein reported arriving KTEB July 6, 2019, 17:20, flight N212JE. The notification that set up the July 6 arrest. Feb 13, 2020 attorney proffer (EFTA00025091, 7 pp.): SDNY's written reply to follow-up questions from a February 13, 2020 attorney proffer by a former Epstein staff member (identity redacted in the text read; later Maxwell's assistant). The profferer's account as stated: hired via a headhunter after a missed New York Knicks event-planning opportunity; interviewed by Maxwell, then Epstein; trained by Epstein's then-assistant; told massages were part of Epstein's normal day ("Akin to someone going daily to the gym"); Maxwell told her not to fraternize with Epstein's business associates/friends; Epstein reprimanded her for breaking that rule at a party. The account is the profferer's, not adopted as fact. Austria landing, March 22, 2019 (EFTA00037299, 2 pp.): USMS email, June 18, 2019 — Austria confirmed Epstein's plane, Epstein himself, and three females landed and spent a couple of hours in Austria on March 22, 2019 (via the Vienna RSO). A concrete pre-arrest travel datapoint; no names or ages are stated in the email. Also a P7 extension. Nov 30, 2021 prep notes (EFTA00016590, 3 pp.): a former Epstein employee recalls an address book at the Palm Beach residence (dark blue or black, "a bit bigger than a big iPhone, very thick," an estimated 200–300 pages, blank cover, black ink on white paper), first seen around 1994–1995, "different from the Rolodex." Single-witness recollection, not corroborated by a produced book in DS8. Bank productions: JPMorgan Chase production letter, November 23, 2021 (EFTA00014877 — production sent via FIR, SDNY letter attached); Deutsche Bank production follow-up, November 20, 2019 — a mislabeled file in the prior day's submission, corrected copy attached (EFTA00015935). Both are production legs in the criminal investigation, not the civil settlement (P27). Evidence logs: FD-1087 collected-item logs, March–April 2021 (EFTA00029895, EFTA00029899, 4 pp. each) — massage tables (items 1B130–1B134, beige/brown/peach/green) split from FBI Miami case 31E-MM-108062 to 50D-NY-3027571. Evidence-inventory class. Case-build admin: call with the U.S. Attorney's Office for the Northern District of Georgia re Epstein, March 7, 2019 (EFTA00009889 — pre-indictment coordination); "JE, first_indictment.docx" circulated July 1, 2019 (EFTA00030237 — indictment drafting leg, the day before filing); LSJ tip email, August 12, 2019, 23:04 (EFTA00037809, 3 pp.) — an island worker's tip passed to the FBI the day of the search ("Bluebeach, (Old life guard stand)… should hold some financial records, if it wasn't wiped"; Epstein's office in American Yacht Harbor "should have a lot of info as well") — a single tip, no follow-up in DS8; "Epstein Co-conspirator pies" email, August 19, 2019 (EFTA00037630 — attachment-only stub; the attachment is not in the text layer).

v3.2 (WS5) extension — OCR-tolerant sweep legs: EFTA00090773 (DS9, 39 pp): FD-302, Jul 23, 2019 (SOD-NY-3027571, Serial 168): witness interview recording that Epstein said porn websites "have the best poses for models" and that email addresses Epstein used were jepstein@yahoo.com and joepstein@yahoo.com. EFTA00028785 (DS9, Jul 12, 2019): SDNY letter to Judge Berman (19 Cr. 490). EFTA00095871 (DS9, Jul 11, 2019): Reid Weingarten (Steptoe) letter to Judge Berman.

Endpoint: Nov 2020 OPR Executive Summary. Findings as stated in the summary: OPR did NOT find professional misconduct by Acosta or the other subjects in resolving the investigation through the NPA, and did not find evidence the decision was based on corruption or impermissible considerations such as Epstein's wealth, status, or associations; Acosta resisted defense efforts to return the matter to the state and refused to eliminate incarceration and sex-offender registration; the Oct 2007 "breakfast meeting" did not lead to the NPA, signed weeks earlier. The full OPR report is not in DS8 — only the executive summary and the draft's processing trail; findings are reported as the summary states them. 2Declared NPA breach, June 12, 2009Probable

Verification: DG

Span: 2009–2019. Anchors: EFTA00230786 (Jun 12, 2009 SDFL "willfully violated Term 8" letter, 1,131 pp). Endpoint: the Jun 12, 2009 breach declaration (document-verified). Dated stopping point: Jun 12, 2009 (EFTA00230786) is the endpoint; the documented record runs to Sep 26, 2011 (EFTA00205768) — the 2010–2011 investigation is now its own story, P46. DOWNGRADED in v3.0 (WS4): confirmed → probable. Two v2.1 anchors do not support the story and were removed — EFTA00039665 (May 21, 2021 email forwarding a Knight Capital / SEC whistleblower appeal; nothing about the NPA, breach, or federal inaction) and EFTA00039367 (Sep 17, 2019 BOP psychological-reconstruction memo; post-death, nothing about the 2009 breach or the 2009–2019 period). The "no federal prosecution until the July 2019 case" half has no supporting anchor at all and is bounded here as an unanchored claim, not a verified finding — it rests on the slice + prior findings, not a search of all federal dockets. v3.1 narrows the title/endpoint to the breach declaration alone. A confirmed tier for that narrowed story would be defensible on its two anchors, but the tier is kept probable pending review — the v3.0 downgrade was the correction, and re-tiering it is a review decision, not taken unilaterally in a fix pass.

Open lead (partly answered in v3.3 — see P46): whether no federal prosecution followed the breach declaration until the July 2019 case, and why — the "decade of inaction" question. For 2010–2011 it is now answered in part: a new criminal investigation with victim interviews was opened (Jul–Oct 2010) and a preliminary investigation ran to Mar 2011, then the USAO declined to move forward (P46). Whether anything followed between late 2011 and the 2018–2019 case is still not established in the material read, and the question is kept open for that period. Links: 1, 3. v2.0 DS8 extension: EFTA00013587 documents the victim-notification mechanics whose failure OPR later called poor judgment (see new Story 25, the OPR investigation — the accountability counterpart).

Stat. — a 1-page negotiation terms sheet. v3.3 (WS1) extension — Wild decisions + joint statement + notification record (record extended to Apr 15, 2021): In re Wild, 994 F.3d 1244 (11th Cir. 2021) (en banc, Apr 15, 2021) (EFTA00074599, 69 pp.) — held the CVRA "did not create a private right of action authorizing a crime victim to file stand-alone civil lawsuit to enforce CVRA rights"; petition denied; district court had found a CVRA violation (359 F. Supp. 3d 1201) but after Epstein's death the requested remedies were denied and the action dismissed (41 F. Supp. 3d 1321); dissent by Branch, J., joined by Martin, Jill Pryor, Hull, JJ. May 28, 2020 — email re In re Wild, 955 F.3d 1196 (11th Cir. 2020) (panel) (EFTA00016451, DS8, 1 p.): CVRA rights "do not attach until criminal proceedings have been initiated against a defendant, either by complaint, information, or indictment"; because the government never filed charges and entered an NPA, "the CVRA was never triggered." Joint Statement of Undisputed Facts (CVRA case) (EFTA00191264, 132 pp.) — stipulated facts: (1) between ~2001–2006 Epstein "sexually abused [and] enticed into prostitution minor girls" at his West Palm Beach mansion and elsewhere, incl. Jane Does #1 and #2; §2422 violations; (2) 2006 FBI opened the investigation at PBPD's request; girls 13–17; (4) Jun 7, 2007 FBI hand-delivered a CVRA victim notification letter to Jane Doe #1 promising "best efforts" incl. "reasonable right to confer" and "to be reasonably heard at any public proceeding… involving plea"; (5) Aug 11, 2007 same for Jane Doe #2; (6) Jane Doe #2 was represented by counsel paid for by Epstein; "openly hostile to the investigation… told investigators that she was not a victim… Epstein was an 'awesome man'… would consider marrying Epstein… actively avoided law enforcement… contacted other potential witnesses and victims to advise them against cooperating." (The text layer shows strike-throughs — a redlined draft of the joint statement; cite the stipulated facts as stated.) DRAFT government reply to petitioners' summary-judgment motion (EFTA01100373, 5 pp.): "Any failure to notify or consult were not the result of any 'conspiracy' with JE, nor any bad faith by the Government: they were the result of the USAO following the nationwide DOJ policy" reinforced by a Dec 17, 2010 OLC memorandum opinion (CVRA rights attach when criminal proceedings are initiated; cease if charges dismissed or government declines to bring formal charges after complaint); CVRA amended May 29, 2015 (§3771(a)(9) — right to be informed of a plea bargain/deferred prosecution agreement; "still does not clearly require CVRA rights to apply in the absence of a federal plea agreement or in circumstances where there is a federal Non-Prosecution Agreement"). A DRAFT — cite as the draft reply's argument. All are Deutsche Bank Trust Company Americas 'Not-For-Profit Organization Resolution — Deposit Account' forms, authorizing designated officers to sign, endorse, and draw drafts, checks, and wire/electronic payment orders without limit as to amount. Account-opening paperwork for Gratitude America Ltd at Deutsche Bank; no allegation attached — paperwork only. Count flag RESOLVED (v3.4 WS1 + WS5): 20 Deutsche DBTCA "Not-For-Profit Organization Resolution — Deposit Account" forms in DS10. Publication changes are disclosed in its publication note; coverage tables retain historical survey figures and are labeled accordingly. Later survey scope and limitations are described in the v3.4 entries; no cumulative v3.4 coverage percentage is asserted.

WHAT THIS MAP COVERS

Coverage and limits

Historical coverage table. The supplied table retains v3.0/v3.2 survey figures and the older story-count headline. These are preserved as historical accounting, not current totals. This release has 79 stories and 123 fragments; later survey scope appears in the v3.4 entry updates. No cumulative v3.4 coverage percentage has been established.

Coverage source text

Methods differ per slice — capped FTS hit rows vs docs read in full vs rows surfaced — so cross-set percentage comparisons are not valid; the table says so instead of forcing false precision. "Unsampled remainder" means NOT EXAMINED, CONTENT UNKNOWN — never "story-free." A slice whose method was capped FTS sweeps cannot distinguish "no story" from "story not yet found" in its unsampled remainder.

Set Docs total Sampled or anchored (method) Image-only, unmappable from text Unsampled remainder (not examined)
DS1–7 + DS12 4,253 ~75 read directly (all DS6+DS7, DS3 largest caption docs, DS4/DS12 anchors, speckle checks) + full metadata inventory of all docs in the group ≥3,216 no-text (3,301 for DS1–8+12 combined, less DS8 ~85); speckle-text photo docs in DS1/DS2/DS5 add uncounted unmappable ~4,178 not read directly
DS8 10,594 (29,348 pages) Inventory and expanded survey in v2.0: full metadata inventory of all 10,594 docs; ~2,820 examined at opening depth or better (A ~300, B all 2,110 first-600-char openings + 59 Bates-led reads, C ~120, D ~163, E ~130); ~200 read in full, ~300 more in substantial part/excerpt; ~150 FTS sweep terms per range in places ~85 (99.2% with text) ~7,420 not examined beyond metadata (v3.2 WS2 deeper survey: ~360 eye-read, ~30 read in full; cumulative ~3,170 examined; content unknown, never story-free); includes 414 "No Images Produced" placeholders (range D) and 5 image-only runs totaling 585 pages (range C)
DS9 531,282 (1,223,777 pages) ≤3,240 capped FTS hit rows (upper bound, NOT a distinct-doc count; distinct sampled IDs fewer, never deduplicated); 0 docs read in full; 15 anchor docs verified for existence/size only, content reused from prior findings 53,822 exact (10.1%; EFTA011xxxxx 21,953 + EFTA012xxxxx 16,079) At least 528,042 never even a sampled hit row; sampled-but-unchained remainder likewise unmapped
DS10 503,154 (950,101 pages; 493,068 with text) FEDWIRE chaining in v3.0 (WS3): 1,632 FEDWIRE docs inventoried (7,312 pp.); 161 FirstBank advice instances enumerated advice-by-advice (v3.2 WS4 correction: 170); 1,761 NC-bearing statement lines parsed (345 counterparties; v3.3 WS4 correction: 1,852 genuine occurrences, LSJ lines 316 → 329); ~110 docs examined at opening depth or better; 12 read in full; ~40 verification reads. 917 'STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT' docs connected by counts only in v3.0; chained by account in v3.2 WS1 (20,425 pp.; 1 new probable story, 9 extensions, 10 fragments) ~10,086 (503,154 − 493,068; 20/20 no-text sample = 1-page, 1-image, Bates-only) Effectively the whole slice minus the chained FEDWIRE population and ~35 cited docs; the ~330 smaller-counterparty chains beyond F-WS3-15 inventory, and the ~10,086 no-text docs (the 917 statements are now chained, v3.2 WS1)
DS11 331,655 (517,382 pages) ~700 distinct docs surfaced, ~100 read closely, ~60 anchored (v1) + v3.0 late-Bates re-sweep (WS1): 4,130-doc cluster EFTA02720000–02730262 inventoried; 4,092 examined at opening depth or better (100% of with-text docs); 56 read in full or substantial part; 44 FTS terms × 11 windows 2,780 (99.2% with text) ~327,500 docs outside the late-Bates cluster remain unmapped except for v1's ~700 surfaced docs; the 38 no-text docs in the cluster and the 88 victim-list spreadsheet pages (headers only, by design) are characterized, not surveyed
House Oversight 23,160 pages ~250–280 pages directly read (94 part-opening reads + ~90 direct anchor reads + reused 94-page dedup sample); everything else is FTS-tally evidence, NOT read evidence. v3.2 WS5 assessment: +231 systematic + 41 targeted pages read; 0 HO-only stories found; full pass NOT launched Not applicable in the same way (pages, not docs); 3,534 sub-800-char pages + 51 zero-char pages are fragment/cover material ~22,600 pages not directly read (approximation only; v3.2 adds 272 pages read)
Barak mailbox 43,322 rows → 31,704 canonical messages (main 31,718 + Hyperion 1,621 + part01 4,192 + part02 3,979 + part03 1,812) Sweep-and-anchor pass (~45 FTS terms across all DBs, top-sender/subject scans); ~70 anchor rows body-read in full (v1) + v3.0 WS2 survey of parts 02/03 (~80 sweep terms, ~35 anchor rows body-read). IA Barak parts 02/03 INDEXED in v3.0 (5,791 rows; 11 truly new messages) Not applicable (rows are emails, not scanned pages); 8,942 rows (20.6%) undated across all five DBs v3.2 WS3: pre-2013 mailbox inventoried (2,299 dated main rows) + 2 new probable stories (Sep 2012 arrangement; Bendheim/Nowforce) + SAIL/SPIEF main-DB follow-ups closed; Tiomkin/AT Global resolved as an investigated non-story; Bendheim/PHC resolved (the 198-row "I'm downstairs" cluster is one misfiled forward duplicated); attachments (PDF/DOC/images) not indexed in any part; mailbox dated range ends 2016-02-15; DS11 Sep-2012 sweep still owed

Coverage headline (v3.2): Atlas v3.2 surveys a small, stated fraction of the corpus (anchored documents now numbering in the high hundreds, plus the DS11 late-Bates inventory and expanded survey, the DS10 FEDWIRE chaining and statement chaining, the Barak parts 02/03 indexing, and the pre-2013 mailbox inventory) and labels everything else unsampled-and-unknown, image-only, or duplicate. It makes NO claim about whether unsampled documents contain stories. The story count (76) is the number of stories v3.2 could confirm or probabilistically chain from surveys (72 from v3.1 plus 4 new). Further stories may emerge from unsampled material; their number is unknown.

Unmapped remainder

  • Image-only, unmappable from text: ~70,000 docs (DS9 53,822; DS10 ~10,086; DS11 2,780; DS1/DS2/DS5 photo strata; DS8 ~85). Their stories, if any, live in the pictures — not in this atlas.
  • Duplicates, not new stories: DS9↔DS11 email duplicates; HO internal duplicates (19,626 pages ≥800 chars → 19,314 distinct texts, 307 duplicate groups); Barak internal duplicates (43,322 rows → 31,704 canonical messages in v3.0, WS2 — the v2.1 stored-hash figure 34,801 was inflated by a pipeline entity-decoding artifact; Hyperion adds 5 messages to main, part01 adds 11, parts 02/03 add 11); DS11 internal mailbox-export duplicates (e.g. Jun 14, 2014 Wexner note at EFTA02357422 and EFTA02358529).
  • Bulk material examined in samples that fits no arc under the definition: routine staff/aviation/household correspondence (DS11 is mostly this), newsletters and alerts (RelSci alone: 491 main + 82 part01 Barak rows), single filings, calendar alerts, mega-statement runs with no arc (Amex Epstein 9,824 pp.; Bear Stearns 632 pp.), 917 DS10 "STATEMENT OF ACCOUNT" docs (chained by account in v3.2, WS1), 1,632 DS10 FEDWIRE docs (chained by beneficiary account in v3.0, WS3), 3,906 HO jeevacation pages, 3,534 short/cover/fragment HO pages, phone-record and evidence-inventory bulks (DS4, DS12). Bulk material NOT examined is unknown, not arc-free.
  • Sampled, not censused: DS9 (≤3,240 capped hit rows, zero docs read in full), DS11 (~700 docs surfaced in v1 + 4,130-doc late-Bates cluster inventoried in v3.0), DS10 (~300 snippet rows + ~35 distinct cited docs in v1 + 1,632 FEDWIRE docs inventoried and chained in v3.0), HO (~250–280 pages read), Barak (sweep-and-anchor pass, ~70 rows body-read in v1 + ~35 in v3.0 WS2). The unsampled majority is UNMAPPED, not "story-free." DS8 in v2.0: full metadata inventory done; ~3,170 examined at opening depth or better (v2.0 ~2,820 + v3.2 WS2 ~350 new); ~7,420 not examined beyond metadata (mostly routine SDNY emails + placeholders) — content unknown, not story-free. DS8 was the manageable full-pass target (10,594 docs, completable in one pass, unlike DS9/DS11) — it is NOT the largest unmapped block in absolute terms: DS9's unsampled remainder (≥528,042) and DS11's (≈328,000) are far larger. DS11's late-Bates EFTA0272xxxx–0273xxxx cluster was re-swept in v3.0 (WS1: 4,130 docs inventoried, 4,092 examined at opening depth or better, 56 read in full; DS11 outside the cluster, ~327,500 docs, remains unmapped except for v1's ~700 surfaced docs). Methods differ per slice — capped FTS hit rows vs docs read in full vs rows surfaced — so cross-set percentage comparisons are not valid.

  • Indexed in v3.0 (WS2): IA Barak parts 02/03 (5,791 rows; 11 truly new messages). Resolved in v3.2 (WS3): Barak pre-2013 mailbox inventoried (2,299 dated main rows); Tiomkin/AT Global resolved as an investigated non-story (a 2010–2016 press-forward channel; the one "Epstein" hit is Jason Epstein, a different person); Bendheim/PHC resolved (2 new stories/fragments + the misfiled-forward cluster). Still unmapped: SAIL attachments (MOU/Agreement drafts); the DS11 Sep-2012 sweep; Nowforce follow-through outside the mailbox; Barak attachments (PDF/DOC/images) in any part.

  • v3.2 sweep remainders (v3.3 candidates): NONPROSECUTION 95.0% unsampled (2,495 of 2,627; the 5.0% sample yielded 9+ legs); FIRSTBANK 79.9% unsampled (521 of 652; the 20.1% sample yielded the P9 leg and two P40 legs); BARAK 470 non-co-token docs not individually read; DS8 ~7,420 metadata-only docs remain (machine-clustered, not eye-read).

EXPLICIT OPEN ENDS

  • Nidal / “Ronen Cohen”: whether they are different people is not established. The Barak-traveled-as-Cohen hypothesis remains unproven.
